# RUNLOG.md — overnight run, 2026-11-16

One line per event, written before the event is acted on (FLEET_PACK.md §10.1 R9).
Format: `time · ticket · model · event`.

States a ticket can end in: **MERGED-GREEN** (gate exit 0 after merge) or **PARKED** (written reason in
PARKED.md). Nothing else. A ticket with no line has not been reached.

## Reading notes for the owner

- **"No commits" is read as: the four run-record files are not committed.** RUNLOG.md, PARKED.md,
  TBD.md and AUDIT.md stay uncommitted in the working tree so you read them as working-tree files.
  The ticket merges of §10.2 R6 are the plan itself and are committed on `main`. Nothing is pushed
  (R8). If you meant no commits at all, every ticket below is still on its `t/T-XXX` branch and
  `git log` shows exactly what landed.
- **D-064** was written before the first dispatch: the 14 enemy-system rules are FINAL as proposed,
  plus the Isagi ruling (volley gap AND 4 s dwell limit, both) and the nuke ruling (salvo ≤ 6 s,
  props/spires/bastion walls/tile slabs block it).
- **The 17 enemy images are not in `assets_src/enemies/`.** Every enemy sprite tonight is a TBD
  placeholder. See TBD.md.
- Budget: at most 10 Inkling 2 Small, 3 GLM 5.3 Flash, 3 DeepSeek V4.1 including the orchestrator.
  Tonight's stricter rule wins over §1: a twice-failed Inkling ticket is split first, parked if it
  cannot split — it never goes to GLM.

02:34Z · T-022 · orchestrator · METHODOLOGY FLAG FOR THE OWNER - READ THIS WITH THE MERGE. To get A11 green the worker changed the AUDIT'S SIMULATION MODEL, not just the economy numbers. Four changes: (a) "affords all 3" went from "three individually affordable" to "the three prices SUM to <= purse"; (b) the average player went from "buys every affordable offer in order" to "buys up to the two most expensive affordable offers"; (c) the cautious player went from "buys the cheapest affordable" to "buys the cheapest only when price x 3 <= purse"; (d) income went from linear interpolation between rows to "nearest lower row". The canon bands (>=80% / <=30% / 18-24 / 6-10) are UNTOUCHED, which is the important part. But a model tuned until it lands inside a band proves nothing by itself, and the code comment literally says the buying policy is "the policy that matches the 18-24 purchases band" - that is circular. Each change is individually defensible (carrying the nearest lower income row invents no values; the sum reading makes the 30% cap mean something; buying up is realistic), but collectively they were tuned. I told the worker to justify each one from the spec or realism independently of the band it helps hit, and to say plainly if a change was chosen mainly to move a number into band. The owner should decide whether to trust the model or the bands.

06:19Z · telegraph lifetime · orchestrator · PARKED, NOT FIXED, AND I WANT TO BE PLAIN ABOUT WHY. T-032 and T-034 independently found that enemy_runtime.telegraph() stores ticks and total but nothing ever decrements or removes the entry, so rt.telegraphs grows for the whole run and the arbiter treats every telegraph ever fired as still live - which can block address_me's no_targeted_telegraph requirement at a spot for the rest of the run. I tried to fix it centrally twice and reverted both times. Decrementing ticks each step double-decrements coil's ring (it ages its own and asserts exactly 0 at the landing tick, D-T072-4) and broke test_enemy_coil.gd. Pruning on birth + total deletes nuke's ring mid-descent, because nuke passes TRACK_TICKS (150) as the duration while the ring must stay visible through the 90-tick descent (240 total), and broke test_enemy_nuke.gd. There is no single rule that is correct for both merged behaviors, so enemy_runtime.gd is exactly as T-027 left it and the defect is PARKED row 9 with both attempts and both failure outputs recorded. The owner has to decide whether behaviors own their footprint lifetime or the runtime does. This is the honest outcome: I did not ship a change that broke two green tickets to make a third one's gate easier.

02:21Z A8 RE-VERIFIED as a false positive, this time against the full report rather than the summary. The claim was that done rings are appended to `keep` forever and the view draws a stale 14 u band for up to 4.0 s. `_step_ring_once` is called every tick from `tick()`'s `for _i in range(dt_ticks)` loop, and its first statement skips any ring with `done` set, so a completed ring is dropped on the very next tick - one tick at r=14, which is correct, not a cooldown. The view then sees an empty list and hides the band. No change made; the false-positive record stands.

21:02Z ROOT CAUSE 1 (the load-bearing one, found by experiment not by reading): `view/level_mesher.gd` `_face_y()` had its two winding branches SWAPPED. The `up=true` branch was back-facing from above, so every tile TOP was culled and the player was looking at the slab UNDERSIDE (`slab_side`, dark, untextured). Proven three ways: forcing TOP_SHADER ALBEDO magenta changed nothing, forcing SIDE_SHADER green turned the whole "floor" green, and swapping the two branches made the tops appear. The same helper draws prop tops and stems, so those were culled too. The ramp wedge top (`_wedge`) had the same defect in its own quad order. Both fixed.
21:03Z ROOT CAUSE 2: `view/billboard.gd` `setup_from_sheet()` read only the legacy T-016 `animations[]` list, but the sim drives the T-083 `anim.states` names - and 9 of 12 enemies had sim states with no matching art animation (address_me, coil, gift_larper, gin, kiwi_cat, nuke, seal, thicc_ben, toucher), so they froze on whatever frame they last showed. Both lists are merged now. RECORDED PLAINLY: the toucher BODY was never broken - a direct render shows the gold hand. The black/magenta checker the owner saw is `floor_00..04.png`, the glitched-floor decal derived from their own `assets_src/enemies/glitchedground.jpeg`. That is supplied art, not a missing texture.
21:04Z FIXED (the rest): `HUB_SHADER` was a flat fill and now carries the full detail stack (checker, per-texel grain, per-tile tone shift, seam, bevel, three-octave value noise, noise-derived normal and roughness) while keeping the dais star sigil. `app/world.gd` builds a third-person player figure, a follow camera, a `player_fill_light` omni and a `VoidGlow` DirectionalLight3D (shadows off); `data/palette.json` gained the `void_glow` role and raised `ambient.energy` 1.0 -> 2.6 and the fill light 18 u/1.2 -> 26 u/1.5 - enough to read the tile texture, not enough to flatten the fog. The toucher's glitched floor is now a separate world-origin node (`view/toucher_view.gd`, floor-only mode) because the sim's glitched entries carry absolute tile coordinates while the body node moves. Phasing through a platform was already fixed by PR #1 (prop tops are landing candidates, `test_classes.gd:126`). Gifts were drawn all along - on an untextured floor they could not be seen.
21:05Z TWO NEW TESTS pin the silent defects: `test_mesh.gd` `_winding()` asserts every top-material triangle's geometric outward normal points up and no `slab_side` triangle does (Godot front faces are clockwise, so the outward normal of (v0,v1,v2) is (v2-v0)x(v1-v0) - the reverse of the usual right-hand rule, documented in the test); `test_billboard.gd` `_anim_states()` asserts every sim animation state resolves, with a frameless state legal because it holds the previous texture.

22:35Z THE OTHER READING WAS BUILT FIRST AND MEASURED, THEN REVERTED. "Camera trails the character's facing" is unstable by construction: input is rotated by the rig yaw, the character faces its velocity, and the camera chases that facing, closing the loop yaw -> input -> velocity -> facing -> yaw. A scratch probe driving the real rig and the real capture measured **696.6 deg of yaw swept in 2 s of held lateral input (~350 deg/s)** with the camera never catching up (worst behind-dot -0.097, i.e. nearly side-on). That is a spinning top, not a camera lock, so the rig changes were reverted with `git checkout -- game/app/camera_rig.gd` rather than shipped with a smoothing constant tuned to hide it. The probe was a scratch file and is deleted; the numbers are in D-T082-4 so nobody re-derives them.

Replaced in both `world.gd` and `test_gift_larper_mines.gd`.

11:49Z THE DIFFICULTY BANDS ARE A BOT ARTIFACT, NOT A GAME DEFECT - the most important finding of the day. Deaths by code: D-MINE 37, D-KIWI 24, D-COIL 17, D-YOU 14, D-VOID 6, D-SEAL 1. At level 5, 34 of 39 deaths are tripmines (87%), and 34% of ALL runs end on a mine at level 5. Enemy votes at level 5 are only 2, so density is not the cause. THE BOT HAS NO MINE MODEL AT ALL: `bot_run.gd` calls `MinesScript.tick()` to advance the sim and never reads a mine position. Three more bot gaps: it dodges the rumble instead of YOU?!'s ring (the ring outlives its telegraph by 94 ticks), it uses a 1.5 u vertical contact band against the game's 1.8 u body, and enemy velocity is keyed by content id so two copies of the same enemy corrupt each other's velocity. All four are TOOL gaps. CONCLUSION: do not tune enemy data to chase these bands until the bot can see mines - the measurement is not yet measuring the game. One real game-side defect was found and reported: `rt.telegraphs` is append-only and never pruned while `_live_telegraphs()` scans it linearly.

12:24Z THE BOT NOW HAS A MINE MODEL. `bot_run.gd` treats every tile in a live mine's column as lethal ground (`MINE_COL_COST`) and keeps its centre clear of the 0.9 u trigger cylinder. It also dodges the ring rather than the rumble, uses the real 1.8 u body band, and keys enemy velocity per instance instead of per content id. `rt.telegraphs` is no longer append-only: the live scan does not walk the whole history, and `test_telegraph_bound.gd` pins that the list stays bounded while a live footprint still blocks.
13:10Z THE BANDS RE-MEASURED WITH A PROBE THAT CAN SEE THE GAME, and the answer is that the probe cannot reach them. Two probe defects were found and both fixed. (a) MINES: D-MINE went from 37 of 99 deaths to ZERO once the bot modelled a live mine's column as lethal ground. (b) VOID: D-VOID went from 5 of 20 runs to ZERO once the bot stopped routing over unsupported tiles. Together they took level 6 reach from 4% to 15% and max level from 6 to 8. **Every remaining death is now a genuine enemy kill** - D-COIL 8, D-KIWI 6, D-YOU 4, D-TOUCH 1 - so the probe is no longer blind to anything; it is simply losing fights. A3 still PASSES: level 1 completed 19/20, median 41.5 s.
13:11Z WHY THE BANDS STILL FAIL, and why I did NOT tune enemy data. Per-level survival is 95 / 84 / 88 / **43** / **33** percent. Levels 3 and 4 have the same curse count and the same mine count; the only difference is ONE MORE ENEMY BODY, and survival drops 45 points. Reaching 80% at level 6 needs about 96% survival on each of levels 1-5 (0.956^5 = 0.80). No enemy-data change closes a 53-point gap on one level and a 63-point gap on the next. The one band-safe change the analysis found - move the third enemy vote from lobby 3 to lobby 5 in `game/data/offering_schedule.json`, which preserves every DESIGN section 9 row (3 votes entering level 6, 6 entering 12, 9 entering 18) and would take level 6 to roughly 48% - is recorded in PARKED.md row 31 and NOT applied. Tuning the game to satisfy an instrument that cannot play it is exactly the mistake the mine model already exposed. **Owner decision needed:** apply that schedule change, or accept the bands as a target for a competent human that this probe cannot validate. No band was moved and no enemy data was changed.

15:07Z CRUMBLING COULD NEVER FIRE, AND NOW IT CAN. A worker wired it completely and then proved the shipped game has no 1-wide walkway: GEN_VERSION 4 (owner override J1) makes every walkway exactly three cells wide, and a 12-level x 5-seed sweep found zero qualifying tiles. I ruled it reads as the outer lane - void on one side, floor on the other - because changing the generator would break the owner's override, parking it would ship a brutal curse that does nothing, and the edge reading is the only one under which the counter-play sentence "keep moving; never returns to a soft-lock because they regrow" is true. Measured 55-57% of walkable tiles, above my own 50% guardrail, and I shipped it anyway: a tile is 0.5 u and the player runs 9 u/s, so crossing one takes 3.3 ticks against a 72-tick fall delay. Moving is safe, stopping is fatal. A 3-wide walkway becomes a tightrope with a surviving centre spine. The guardrail was a hypothesis about the game and the measurement disproved the hypothesis, not the arithmetic.
15:08Z WET FLOOR WAS FREE, AND NOW IT IS NOT. `friction_mult` returned 0.4 for a glitched tile OR a slippery one, so the curse's entire effect was already in the base game: a worker measured the coast distance on a live glitched tile as IDENTICAL with the curse held and not held (1.0500 vs 1.0500). DESIGN section 7 (D-075) says the opposite - the glitched floor's slow is 20% for everyone and Wet Floor adds only the x0.4 slipperiness. The sim now follows the spec: `friction_mult` reads only the slippery map, and the -20% run speed is applied independently by `run_speed()` and untouched, so the toucher's threat is unchanged. After: held 1.0500 vs not held 0.3750. This is a balance change to a shipped enemy effect made without the owner, it is one line, and it is reversible in one line. Logged in DECISIONS.md as needing confirmation.

15:35Z THE FRESH-EYES REVIEW FOUND A REAL PRIVACY LEAK, AND IT WAS MINE TO FIX. 15:36Z TWO THINGS WERE WRONG AND BOTH ARE FIXED. The file was tracked at all, when the standing rule is that it stays uncommitted - so my own instruction to myself ("keep GUARDIAN.md out of the commit") was based on a false premise, and not adding it to a commit did nothing because it was already tracked from an earlier commit. And it carried an absolute path from the machine it runs on. dc26fb3 scrubs the working tree to the same `C:\Users\username` placeholder the rest of the repo uses, untracks the file and gitignores it so it cannot be published again. The file itself is kept on disk at 123,630 bytes - nothing is deleted. A re-scan of every tracked file now finds zero non-placeholder profile segments.
15:37Z WHAT I CANNOT FIX, AND WHY. The name is still in the history of earlier commits, including the blob on origin/main. Removing it from history needs a rewrite, and this project forbids history rewrites. So the leak is already public and the remedy is the owner's call: either accept it, or authorise a history rewrite, or make the repository private. I am recording it rather than doing it, because a rewrite is exactly the kind of irreversible action the hard limits exist to prevent. This is the one finding of the cycle that needs the owner and cannot be closed by me.
15:38Z THE LESSON IS THE SAME ONE AS THE STALE DOCUMENTS. I had been told, and had repeated to myself, that GUARDIAN.md was the only dirty file and must stay out of commits. Both halves were wrong: it was tracked, and it was dirty because it was being edited, not because it was untracked. A rule about a file's state is a claim about the tree, and claims about the tree need checking. That is now the seventh stale claim this session, and the first one with a privacy consequence.

17:07Z I BROKE THE BOUNDARY SCAN MYSELF, AND THE FIX WAS BETTER THAN THE BUG. Two workers independently reported it red before I ran the gate. The fix was not to reword a comment: each of the four upgrades now carries its own module declaring `static knobs(stacks, kit)`, and `world.gd` merges whatever the owned modules declare, so it names no id at all. That is the shape the project already uses for every other script upgrade, and it puts each number next to the rule sentence that states it. The knob probe reads the same five values as before the refactor.
17:08Z THE GATE'S FIRST RED WAS A TEST THAT COULD NOT FAIL CLEANLY. `run_all` failed on a SCRIPT ERROR from `test_run_log.gd:542`: `JSON.parse_string("")` returns null, and assigning null to a typed Dictionary is a script error. The test still reported `failures=0`, because the crash happened before its asserts ran - so the leg had been silently proving nothing. Four parse sites are now null-safe and return `{}`, which lets the assert speak. That immediately exposed a second, real defect: the log was not readable mid-run at all.
17:09Z THE ATOMIC-SAVE FIX BROKE A CONTRACT, AND THE CONTRACT WON. W14-C correctly made settings.gd and records.gd publish atomically, and applied the same shape to run_log.gd. But the run log is append-only and it is read WHILE it is written - its own header promises that, and `_record`'s per-line flush exists to serve it. A .tmp-then-rename publish makes the destination invisible until close, so the mid-run reader saw nothing. I reverted run_log.gd to direct writes and wrote down why: the failure atomicity guards against here is a truncated tail, which the loader already tolerates, not corruption. The test leg that encoded the wrong contract now asserts mid-run readability instead. This is the one place this cycle where a worker's fix was right in general and wrong in particular, and the difference was a documented promise in the file being changed.
17:10Z THREE REVIEW BUGS AND A FOURTH OF THE SAME SHAPE. BUG-1: a failed level build called `_show_menu()` without `_end_run()`, so the run's bests and its telemetry vanished silently - every other exit path already recorded. BUG-2: RETRY was not re-entrant; the button grabs focus, so a double-click tore down the world the first pass had just built and could leave STATE_WORLD with `_world` null, an empty frame only ESC escaped. BUG-3: the class altar's SKIP emitted a signal nothing connected to, so the button looked live and did nothing. BUG-7, same file and same rule: the last synchronous `_loading.free()` on an in-tree node is now `queue_free`. All four are proven by `test_app_review_fixes.gd` with negative controls.
17:11Z THE STRAY DIRECTORY WAS THREE EMPTY FOLDERS. BUG-11 reported `game/game/out/shots_j13_after/` as untracked junk from a path bug. It held zero files, so I removed it with `rmdir`, which only succeeds on empty directories - nothing could be lost, and the command itself is the proof. The upstream path bug that created it is not fixed; it is a tool resolving its output relative to the wrong cwd, and it will come back if that tool runs again.
17:12Z WHAT IS STILL OPEN. The privacy leak is unchanged and still needs the owner: the profile name is in earlier commit history, and removing it needs a rewrite this project forbids. `test_upgrades_world.gd`'s INERT list is a discovery snapshot whose legs measure effects differently from the authoritative per-upgrade tests, so it over-reports; the authoritative proofs are `test_upgrade_hooks.gd` and `test_upgrade_consumers.gd`. 17:14Z THE RELEASE WENT OUT AND THE CI FLAG WAS WRONG FOR THE FOURTH TIME. v0.1.4-alpha.3 is tagged and released with all three assets (voidwrap_run.exe, .x86_64, .zip), and CI again created it as a full release rather than a pre-release. I fixed it by hand within the minute, and then fixed the cause: the workflow never passed `--prerelease` to `gh release create`, which defaults to a full release. The flag is now derived from the tag - a hyphen suffix means pre-release, which is the semver convention - so it needs no list to maintain and cannot drift from the tags it describes. Four manual corrections is not a coincidence, it is a missing argument.
17:26Z THE 261 MS HITCH IS 3X SMALLER AND STILL NOT FIXED. Reproduced at 249 ms at tick 120 on the biggest level (1778 tiles, level 25). The cause: the whole enemy plan spawns on one tick, and that tick paid two one-time costs inside a single 60 Hz step - the enemy runtime's spawn round-trip set, and the first bespoke enemy view build per roster id, which loads frame textures through ResourceLoader. Both are pure functions of the level dict and the roster plan, so both are now paid at build time, before the first step. Views are pre-built one per planned copy and popped off a stack as rows appear; ids with no bespoke view get one art pin per id so their board build is cache hits. Worst step 261 -> 86 ms, and the next slowest step is 8.6 ms, so what remains is one cost rather than a recurring one. Mean 2.54 ms. It is NOT under the 16.67 ms budget: the remaining ~63 ms is in the sim's own spawn tick, not the view pass, and closing it means spreading the spawn itself across ticks - a sim change, not a view change. Recorded as an improvement, not a closed item.
17:27Z WHAT I WOULD DO NEXT, IN ORDER. (1) Spread the enemy spawn across ticks so the last 86 ms goes too - it is the only remaining frame over budget and it is a sim-side change. (3) Reconcile `test_upgrades_world.gd`'s INERT legs against the authoritative per-upgrade tests, or mark the file as a discovery snapshot so nobody reads its count as current. (4) The privacy leak still needs the owner and is the only item I cannot close.

17:45Z THE LESSON IS ABOUT THE BRIEF, NOT THE WORKER. I told W14-D to drive the app's own `_process`, then re-briefed it to fall back to the level loop if the app could not be driven headless. It took the fallback, and the fallback is where it crashed. A 531-line driver written from scratch in one session, with no smaller green step in between, is the shape that fails: the worker never had a two-level run passing before it tried ten. The next attempt should be told to get ONE level green and report, then two, then ten - and to stop at the first crash rather than push through it.
18:34Z NINE UPGRADES WERE TESTED AND NEVER CALLED, AND THAT IS THE CYCLE'S REAL FINDING. A fresh-eyes review of the cycle diff found the same defect the cycle was supposed to have fixed, one layer down: every sim-side consumer existed and had a passing test, and the call site in the shipped path did not. So the tests proved the sim and not the game. `pocket_beacon`, the most expensive upgrade in the game at 1200 g, could never teleport, because its channel was only ever advanced by the bot engine. `bounty_hunter`'s +50% bounty was never shown or paid, because the real Antechamber never ran the draw pass. `more`, `last_call`, `straggler_scope`, `spring_heels`, `twin_dash`, `updraft` and `marathon` were the same shape. All nine are wired now. The lesson is not "write more tests" - there were tests, and they were honest about what they covered. The lesson is that a test which drives the sim directly cannot tell you whether the game calls the sim, and a coverage map that counts consumers instead of call sites will report green while the shop sells nothing.
18:35Z THE ONE UPGRADE THAT STILL DOES NOTHING IS RECORDED, NOT PAPERED OVER. `quick_recovery`'s rule sentence gives the Athletic +5% run speed instead of a cooldown cut. The Athletic has no class cooldown, so the cooldown knob is meaningless for it, and the controller exposes no per-upgrade run-speed knob to add to - `run_speed_mul` belongs to the stat pipeline. The module returns `{}` for that class, which is the same live behaviour as before the module existed. Inventing a knob to make one sentence true is a bigger change than the finding justifies, and doing it silently would be worse than recording it. It is in the module header, in README's known gaps, and in DECISIONS D-C2-5. Closing it properly means giving the stat pipeline a per-upgrade run-speed contribution, which is the next cycle's work and not this one's.

18:48Z THE FINAL REVIEW FOUND THE SAME DEFECT A THIRD TIME, AND IT WAS THE ONE I HAD JUST CLAIMED TO FIX. A fresh-eyes pass over the fix commits rated N1 HIGH: `pocket_beacon` still could not be used by a human. The channel cancelled on `buttons != 0`, and INTERACT is a HELD bit - `input_capture` sets it from `Input.is_key_pressed`, not from an edge - so the press edge started the channel on tick N and the same key, still down, killed it on tick N+1. Only a one-tick tap could ever fill it, which no human can do. So the 1200 g upgrade was inert in practice on the same day I wired it and wrote that it worked. The module's own test never caught it because it drove the sim with tick-perfect inputs: press INTERACT, then 180 ticks of `InputScript.neutral()`. That is a bot's input, not a player's. This is the third instance of one pattern in this cycle - a test that proves the sim and not the game - and the first one that survived my own fix for it.
18:49Z THE FIX IS IN THE SIM, NOT A MASK IN THE APP. The reviewer's suggested patch was to strip INTERACT from the buttons in `world.gd` before calling `step_player`, which would have made the app lie to the sim. The module's own contract says otherwise: its test asserts that BUTTON_ABILITY cancels the channel, never that INTERACT does, and the doc comment's "any input" was written before anyone noticed the channel button is itself an input. So the cancel test now excludes the channel button and nothing else: `var other_buttons := buttons & ~InputScript.BUTTON_INTERACT`. Any other button, any move axis, or leaving the ground still cancels. Two proofs, both with negative controls. The sim leg holds INTERACT for all 180 ticks and expects the teleport; with the fix reverted it fails at tick 1 with "a held INTERACT cancelled the channel". The world leg drives `world.step()` through a `ScriptedWorld extends WorldScript` that overrides `_capture_input()` - the same seam `test_determinism.gd` uses - holds INTERACT for 200 ticks, and asserts the controller lands exactly on the dais centre with `channel_used` set; with the fix reverted it fails with the body still at (1.50, 1.50) against a dais at (0.50, 0.50). The un-owned twin is the control in both. Measured while writing it: the dais sits 1.41 u from the spawn on every level and seed I probed, so the leg's guard is "the spawn is not already the dais centre", not a distance threshold.
18:50Z THE SECOND FINDING WAS A REAL DATA-LOSS PATH, RARE AND CHEAP TO CLOSE. N2 LOW: `_rename_over` moves the destination to `.old` before renaming the `.tmp` over it, so the destination is absent inside that two-rename window. A crash there, or a failed rename followed by a failed restore, left the good bytes in `.old` while `load_from` read only the destination - so the player's settings and bests silently reset to defaults, and the next save overwrote the recoverable copy. The window is two renames wide, which is why it is LOW, but the fix is ten lines: `load_from` now falls back to `.old` then `.tmp` through the same validated parse, and the doc comment says "recoverable" instead of the stronger "never absent" it used to promise. The new leg seeds a good file, moves it aside to simulate the crash, and asserts recovery for both settings and records; the no-files case is the control, so a loader that always returned a stale value would fail. With the fallback removed: 3 failures. With it: 0.

18:58Z THE 30-LEVEL PROOF FINALLY RAN, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT THE HARNESS WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM. The re-dispatch on the replacement model produced a 40-line driver that calls the shipped `BotRun.play_run` entry point instead of reimplementing the loop, and it works: 30 levels, headless, exit 0, a result line per seed. I verified it myself rather than taking the report - seed 38 gives `entered=2 outcome=dead l1_tick=1774 class=glider death_tick=1872`, exit 0, no crash. The previous 531-line driver crashed the engine before its first print; this one is a tenth the size and does not, because it reuses the harness that already exists. That is the whole lesson of the three attempts: the first two tried to build a level loop, and the loop was already there.
18:59Z THE BOT IS THE LIMIT, NOT THE GAME LOOP. Across 14 seeds that produced a result, the bot enters a median of 3-4 levels and dies: entered=2 on seeds 4 and 7, entered=3 on 1, 8, 11 and 14, entered=4 on 3, 5, 6 and 9, entered=5 on 2, entered=6 on 10, entered=7 on 13. Best 7, worst 2, none reached 30. One seed (11) timed out rather than dying. So the intermission-to-next-level handoff is proven end to end at depth, and what is unproven is whether a *player* can go 30 levels - the bot is a simple heuristic and it does not survive the difficulty curve. That is a different ticket from the one I have been chasing, and it is the honest state of the gap. The README now says exactly this instead of "the crash is in the driver's own loop".

19:12Z THE LOBBY'S ENEMY EXHIBIT THREW A SCRIPT ERROR ON EVERY BUILD, AND IT WAS A NULL IN A JSON FILE. W17's sweep caught it in a backtrace: `SCRIPT ERROR: Invalid call. Nonexistent 'float' constructor` at `antechamber.gd:689`, four times per lobby build. The cause is a trap in `Dictionary.get()`: `nuke`'s `sprites.json` declares `"world_height": null`, and `.get("world_height", 1.0)` only falls back to its default when the key is ABSENT - a present null comes back as null, and `float(null)` is exactly that error. The exhibit still appeared because the billboard is added before the throw, so it looked cosmetic; what actually happened is the function aborted, so the exhibit's name came back empty and the body scale was never applied. I reproduced it in isolation (`raw=<null> type=0`, then the error), fixed it with a type-checked read, and proved it both ways: all 12 enemy exhibits build clean with the fix, and reverting it brings the error back with `name=` empty for nuke. The codebase already had the right pattern in `billboard.gd:38`, `coil_view.gd:78`, `menu_stage.gd:542` and `enemy_anim.gd:80` - `antechamber.gd` was the one place that trusted the default. New `test_antechamber_exhibits.gd` builds every registered enemy's exhibit and asserts a non-empty name, with the null-world_height enemy as the explicit control; reverted it fails twice naming nuke.

That was measured on `BotRun.play_run`, which does not shop between levels. The driver I had written off as crashing - W14-D's `old_driver_single_free.gd` - actually works, and it drives the SHIPPED path: real `world.build`, real lobby, real purchases, ledger carried forward. I ran it myself twice. At TARGET_LEVEL 10: `outcome=target_reached deepest=10 completed=9 lobbies=9 ledger_checks=72 purchases=1 stack_types_carried=3 purse=184 class=glider ticks=34480`. At TARGET_LEVEL 30: `outcome=dead deepest=11 completed=10 lobbies=10 ledger_checks=80 purchases=2 stack_types_carried=5 purse=224 class=glider ticks=41621 ms=97272`. So ten consecutive levels are proven end to end through the real intermission loop, with the shop and the carried stacks working, and the bot dies on level 11. The 30-level run is still unproven, but the gap is now precisely "the bot dies on 11" rather than "the harness crashes". The same bot reaches a median of 4 levels without shopping, so shopping between levels roughly doubles its depth - that is a real balance signal, not a bot artefact.

17:33Z "SPAM PLATFORM" IS THE SPAWN PLATFORM, AND I SAID SO IN THE BRIEF RATHER THAN GUESSING SILENTLY. The owner wrote "the only platform that cannot be destroyed is the spam platform". There is no spam platform in this game; there is exactly one dominant spawn platform, built by voidgen.build_hub() and tagged ROLE_HUB. I told the worker that reading, told it to identify the hub by the generator's own role tag and never by coordinates, and told it to report if the identification was impossible. It was possible, so the exemption is structural: the hub's tiles are excluded when the collapse set is built, not filtered out later, which means no future filter can leak one back in.
17:40Z THE ALTAR BUG WAS TWO BUGS AND NEITHER WAS THE ONE I SUSPECTED. I briefed four candidate causes and ranked the look-direction dot product first. The real defects were in the hold-to-confirm bookkeeping: refresh() cleared _holds on every state rebuild, and a Select bumps the stamp and rebuilds every wrapper - so the hold that confirmed a pick was destroyed by the pick itself, and one continuous press confirmed again every 0.35 s. The latch lived on the wrapper dict, which the same rebuild discards, so it never survived either. A shop bay that never resolves would have sold its whole stock on one press. Hold and latch now key on the altar SLOT (bay, altar row, row kind), which is stable across a rebuild, and a released F clears the latch. The lesson is the same one this project keeps teaching: the defect was in the state's lifetime, not in the geometry.
17:41Z THE PROMPT IS NOW THE REFERENCE ART RATHER THAN A LABEL ABOVE THE EXHIBIT. A wide dark-indigo slab with a lighter top edge, a large white capital F as the keybind glyph, the item's name beside it, its description on one line under the name, and a faint accent silhouette at the right end. It crosses the exhibit body instead of floating above it, because the owner asked for the text to overlap the enemy with the name still readable, and it faces the player. The F ramps in scale and colour as the hold fills. A third defect fell out of the same work: altar_details() switched on the BAY kind, but both extra plinths arrive with altar < 0, so every tithe plinth read "REROLL" - only the ROW kind can tell them apart.
17:44Z THE COLLAPSE IS A SIM SCHEDULE AND THE VIEW ONLY READS IT. sim/collapse.gd is pure and tick-driven: on the beacon-open tick every non-hub tile cracks, and 300 ticks (5.0 s at the sim's fixed 60 Hz) later it is written into the collision world's existing "removed" set, which tile_alive() and support_at() already honour. Nothing in the collapse kills a player - a body left without support falls through the controller's own void rule, so there is still exactly one death path. level_mesher.build gained a part argument so the hub and the rest mesh separately; without that split the rest's hidden-face test would see the hub's tiles and leave a hole where the hub used to be.

The glider is a hang glider built from the owner's second reference - a swept delta wing with a bright cyan rim, a keel/crossbar/A-frame skeleton and a rounded pod - built once, parented to the figure, hidden until ctl.gliding, then banking with lateral velocity and pitching with vertical velocity, with a deploy and a fold.

20:06Z THE FIX IS A BUDGET, NOT A RULE CHANGE, AND THAT DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE TICKET. `_spawn_planned()` now spends at most SPAWN_PER_TICK = 4 bodies a tick, consumed in plan order; an entry cut mid-way keeps its `spawned_copies` progress and its place in the queue, so the next tick resumes exactly where it stopped. `pick_spawn_tile`, `_pick_anchor`, `_pick_round_trip`, `_copy_positions` and `_round_trip_cached` are byte-identical - a body spawned on a later tick is picked by the same rule against that tick's inputs. 20:46Z ROW 9 WAS STALE AND I CLOSED IT WITH THE CODE THAT FIXED IT. The row says the sim never expires telegraph footprints, so `rt.telegraphs` grows for the whole run and a stale footprint blocks address_me's SR-4 gate for the rest of the level. That was true when it was written and it is not true now: `_live_telegraphs()` returns only entries with `tick <= now < tick + max(total, 1)`, `arbiter_evaluate()` passes THAT list to `ArbiterScript.evaluate()`, and `_prune_telegraphs()` drops entries 1200 ticks past expiry every 60 ticks so the list is bounded. The two failed attempts the row records are exactly why the fix is a stamped window rather than a decrement or a `birth + total` prune - neither touches `ticks`, so coil still ages its own ring to exactly 0 on the landing tick and nuke still keeps its ring through the descent. The row's "owner decision needed" is obsolete and would have cost the owner a decision they do not have to make.

A parked row whose blocker is "we have not decided what X means" should be re-read after the test suite decides what X means.
20:53Z TWO ROWS CLOSED AS STALE IN ONE PASS, AND THAT IS A SIGNAL ABOUT THE LIST, NOT ABOUT ME. Rows 9 and 30 both described real bugs, both were fixed, and neither row was updated when the fix landed. The list is append-only by convention and nothing forces a fix to come back and close its row, so a stale row is the default outcome rather than the exception. The cheap discipline that would prevent it: whoever fixes a parked row greps PARKED.md for the row id in the same commit. I am not adding a rule for it now - I am writing down that the list needs a sweep before anyone treats it as a queue, which is exactly the mistake I nearly made tonight.

11:20Z B10 LANDED: THE COLLAPSE IS NOW A SHRINKING SAFE ZONE, AND I RE-MEASURED EVERY NUMBER MYSELF INSTEAD OF TAKING THE WORKER'S. The owner's correction supersedes D-B6-2: the level is cut into ZONES by distance from the dais (every non-hub piece, a platform or a bridge, never a single tile, goes into one zone by its NEAREST tile), the farthest zone cracks on the beacon-open tick and falls 5.0 s later, 10.0 s after that the next zone inward cracks, and so on until only the hub is left - one zone cracking at a time. Zone count = clamp(ceil(radius/16 u), 1, 6), so 2 to 6 zones and 35 to 80 s across the 90 measured levels. Every number is in game/data/collapse.json; sim/collapse.gd holds only the rule. The schedule now rides on lstate.collapse and sim/state_hash.gd dumps a COLLAPSE line (absent when a level carries no collapse, so every pre-B10 golden is unchanged). Merged e9cf7b2, gate GATE OK (9 ran, 0 skipped, 488 s), TESTS 124 passed 0 failed, PARITY 56/56, SELFTEST 18/18, GALLERY 16/16, FOGCHECK 550 checks. My own re-runs, not the worker's: zone_reach.gd over seeds 1/7/42 x levels 1-30 gives unwinnable=0, strand_tiles=0/91770, worst_strand_slack=11.00 u (seed 42 level 11), worst_win_slack=311.84 u (seed 42 level 13), zones 2..6, max_dur 80 s; bot --runs 100 --max-level 2 gives level 1 97/100 (bar was >= 95); bot --runs 60 --max-level 18 --seed-base 60717 gives level 6 20.0% (12/60), identical to the pre-B10 baseline. TWO FINDINGS I AM NOT DRESSING UP. (1) The bot numbers CANNOT move and that is not a pass: tools/bot_run.gd drives RunState directly and never builds a world or calls CollapseScript (verified by grep, and its only diff in this commit is comment text), so the collapse has no automated coverage in the bot at all - the owner's "run-inward policy" has nothing to act on until the collapse is wired into the bot, which is a separate ticket. (2) The one consumer that DOES run the collapse, the app-world driver, got worse: it reached level 10 under the old global fall and dies on level 8 now (purse 170 -> 97). The cause is not the collapse being broken - under the global fall the bot was caught 50 u out, fell, spent a charge and respawned on the dais, which teleported it past a kiwi cat; under zones it walks in and the cat kills it 21 u from the dais with both charges in hand. Through the 7 levels both runs complete the purse is 246.28 -> 256.28 (+10.00), so each level pays more and the run is shorter. The real gameplay consequence to watch: enemies on inner rings now live up to 80 s into the gold phase instead of 5 s. D-B10-1 records all of it and marks the 16 u zone width and the cap of 6 as CONTESTABLE (the fleet's choice, not the owner's); 5.0 s and 10.0 s are the owner's numbers. Next: adversarial review by codex gpt-6.1-terra at xhigh, then release v0.1.8-alpha.3 and the screenshot pass of levels 1-3.

It prints a DIAG line (zone count, the cracking zone's tile count, whether its overlay is actually visible) so a blank shot cannot be mistaken for a subtle one. Levels 1, 2 and 3 at seed 7: 3 zones each, zone 0 of 114 / 186 / 91 tiles, overlay_visible=true, mesh present, progress 0.50, any_visible=true. What the frames actually show: the crack network is REAL and visible as a dark Voronoi shard lattice with a cyan core on the teal platforms, but it is SUBTLE against the dark biome - at eye level you read it as texture, not as a warning. That is a look-and-feel note for the owner, not a defect: the owner asked for "visible cracks" and they are visible, just quiet. The zone-0-gone frame is unambiguous - the ring is simply absent and the hub stands alone. Next: the codex gpt-6.1-terra xhigh adversarial review is still running; its verdict gets its own line.
11:35Z THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW FOUND A REAL DEFECT AND I FIXED IT RATHER THAN SHIPPING AROUND IT. codex gpt-6.1-terra at xhigh (STATUS: partial - it did not reproduce the 90-level probe, the driver, the full suite or the gate, and said so) returned five findings. Finding 1 was a genuine state-hash COLLISION, not a style note: the COLLAPSE line carried only phase/start/cur/fallen_n/removed_n, so an app world with the collapse ENABLED and one with it DISABLED hashed identically before the beacon opened and then behaved differently, and the same held for two runs whose collapse.json differed in crack/gap seconds or zone width - all of that is DATA, not a function of the Level, so my "the live fields alone pin every zone's crack/fall tick" comment was simply false. Fixed at 8812dc1: the line now also carries enabled, crack_ticks, gap_ticks and a partition digest computed once at create() (sorted keys per zone, so it does not depend on the Level dict's tile order). New leg 17 asserts each of the five collisions; the negative control is real - reverting the line to the old format gives exactly 5 failures, one per collision, and restoring it gives 0. Gate green after the fix: GATE OK (9 ran, 0 skipped, 473 s), TESTS 124 passed 0 failed, replay determinism OK. Findings 2 and 3 were about honesty, not code, and they were right: "no level becomes unwinnable" is a STRAIGHT-LINE bound (XZ distance at 9 u/s, ignoring jumps, height, routes, enemies and airborne state), not a proof, and the bot percentages are not evidence about the collapse at all because bot_run.gd never runs it. Both are now a dated correction on D-B10-1 rather than a headline. Finding 4 was real too: leg 16 compared mesh-category TOTALS, so a dropped piece and a duplicated one could cancel; it now also checks the piece-level partition (every piece with tiles in exactly one zone, the hub in none). Finding 5 (a tile key shared by two pieces could be meshed twice, because create() dedupes and PART_ZONE filters by piece id) is recorded as a known limitation, not fixed: the reviewer found no evidence generated levels produce such a key and a tile belongs to one piece by construction, so fixing it means API churn for a malformed-level case. The review is committed as tickets/B10.review.md. ONE THING THE OWNER SHOULD KNOW: the v0.1.8-alpha.3 tag points at 42b9037, which is BEFORE this fix, so the released binaries do not carry it. I did not force-move a published tag. Re-cutting is one command if wanted (move the tag to 8812dc1 and let the build re-run), or the next release picks it up.

Enemies: all twelve with what each does and how to beat it, taken from DESIGN.md section 5 rather than invented - kiwi cat hurdle it, coil leave the ring, thicc ben move 4 u off the smoke, Isagi a 2 HU step or jump the release, nuke leave the 5 u sphere or break line of sight, YOU?! jump the ring, gift Larper look before you land, toucher keep height and hurdle it, seal jump on the bark, address me stop for 1.5 s, Claude jump the drums / break the strings / outrun the wind, gin walk out of the patch. ICONS: three SVGs at docs/classes/{dasher,glider,athletic}.svg, authored by codex gpt-6.2-sol at medium effort (the owner's named model) in the style of the five reference images - blocky rounded prisms, one bright cyan outline, flat fills, no gradients, no text. The outline is #7CFFF4, the game's own black-gift rim from game/data/palette.json, and the fills are the four teal roles, so the icons belong to this game and not to a stock set. I did not take the worker's word for them: I rasterized all three through Godot's Image.load_svg_from_string on both a light and a dark background and looked at them, and all three read at 340 px with distinct poses (dash lean with speed streaks, canopy glide, running stride). THE GATE CAUGHT ME AND WAS RIGHT. The first gate run FAILED: test_disclaimer.gd requires the ENEMIES.md disclaimer VERBATIM in README.md and I had capitalised the leading "this" while rewriting the footer. Restored the exact sentence, re-ran the gate, green. That is the argument for gating docs-only changes. I ALSO RE-VERIFIED THE DEPTH NUMBER INSTEAD OF COPYING IT. The old README claimed the driver completes 10 levels and dies on the 11th; B10 changed that and the README had not caught up. I re-ran game/out/run30_appworld.gd and got deepest=8 completed=7 lobbies=7 purchases=1 stack_types_carried=1 purse=97 class=glider, so the README now says 7 levels and carries that exact line. One trap worth recording: run directly, that probe crashes with the known "Unreferenced static string" error and then sits idle at 1.4 s of CPU, which is why my first attempt looked hung for 25 minutes - it has to go through the res://out/one.gd wrapper. I also corrected the stale bot figure from 98/100 to the re-measured 97/100. Gate on the final tree: GATE OK (9 ran, 0 skipped, 482 s), TESTS 124 passed 0 failed, PARITY 56/56, SELFTEST 18/18, GALLERY 16/16, FOGCHECK 550 checks, GATE_EXIT=0.
